The agent spent significant time studying the MATLAB codebase and paper methods before writing a Python conversion script.\n\n---\n\n### 1. Data Loading\n\n**Decision:** Load only `Ephys_Behavior` data files (HDF5-format MATLAB v7.3 `.mat` files) via `h5py`, plus separate `motionEnergy_*.mat` files (MATLAB v5 format) via `scipy.io.loadmat`.\n\n**Why (quoted):** \"The MATLAB metadata files identify the session roster used in different analyses, but the packaged data here is flatter than their original `DataObjects` tree. Session/Subject Selection\n\n**Decision:** Use the 12-session \"Figure 8 context-task ALM roster\" from the MATLAB code, specifically from `code/Scripts/Figure 8/Figure8a_thru_c.m` and `code/DataLoadingScripts/Recording and video/load*_ALMVideo.m`.\n\n**Why (quoted):** \"I'm also using the figure-script session roster rather than 'all files with autowater trials', because the paper's context analyses clearly operate on a curated 12-session subset.\" And: \"I've pinned the session roster for the context task from the manuscript code: the Figure 8 scripts use 12 ALM ephys sessions from `JEB6`, `JEB7`, `EKH1`, `EKH3`, `JGR2`, `JGR3`, and `JEB19`.\"\n\n**Why context cohort (quoted from CONVERSION_NOTES.md):** \"The decoder outputs require behavioral context (`WC` vs `DR`), so I converted the ALM two-context electrophysiology cohort rather than the fixed-delay-only or randomized-delay cohorts.\"\n\nSessions hardcoded with specific probe numbers (e.g., JEB6 probe 2, JEB7 probe 1, etc.), matching the MATLAB loader files.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Trial Filtering (Quality Controls)\n\n**Decision:** Include only `hit | miss` trials where `stim.enable == 0` and `early == 0`. Exclude `no` (ignore) trials.\n\n**Why (quoted from CONVERSION_NOTES.md):** \"Ignore / no-response trials were excluded because the requested `outcome` target is binary correct/incorrect and the paper methods state ignore trials were omitted from analyses.\"\n\nThis matches the paper's MATLAB condition strings like `'R&hit&~stim.enable&~autowater&~early'` and the methods text: \"early lick and ignore trials... were omitted from all analyses.\"\n\n---\n\n### 4. Neural Data Processing\n\n**Alignment:** Spikes aligned to `goCue` event (matching `params.alignEvent = 'goCue'` in MATLAB code).\n\n**Time window:** `[-3.0, 2.5]` seconds relative to go cue.\n\n**Why (quoted):** The agent traced this from the Figure 8 scripts. The default params use `[-2.5, 2.5]` but the context scripts use a wider window.\n\n**Binning:** `DT = 1/100` (10 ms bins), producing 550 timepoints per trial. Spikes are histogrammed into bins, then divided by `DT` to get firing rates.\n\n**Smoothing:** Causal Gaussian kernel with window size 15, using \"reflect\" boundary condition -- a direct port of `mySmooth.m`. The kernel zeroes the first half to make it causal.\n\n**Quality filtering:** Exclude clusters with quality labels in `{garbage, gabrga, noisy, real?}`, matching `findClusters(..., {'all'})`.\n\n**Low firing-rate filtering:** Remove units with mean smoothed PSTH firing rate <= 1 Hz across all condition types. The mean FR is computed across multiple condition masks (all trials, DR hits, WC hits, DR misses, WC misses, etc.).\n\n**Why (quoted):** \"The methods pin the paper's key constraints: ALM ephys only, `goCue` alignment, early/ignore trial omission, low-rate unit filtering, and session-count targets I can use as sanity checks.\"\n\n---\n\n### 5. time_from_go_cue Input\n\n**Decision:** A single continuous input variable: the time axis relative to go cue onset.\n\n**Implementation:** For each trial, `input = time[None, :]` where `time = edges[:-1] + DT/2`, spanning `[-3.0, 2.5]` s. Shape is `(1, 550)`.\n\n**Why:** The task instructions specify \"Time from go cue onset in seconds (continuous, time-varying)\" as the decoder input. **No additional reasoning was provided** beyond following the specification.\n\n---\n\n### 6. lick_direction Output\n\n**Decision:** Encode the animal's **actual lick direction** (not the instructed/cued side). Left = 0, right = 1.\n\n**Implementation:** On `hit` trials, the response matches the instructed side (`R=1` means right lick). On `miss` trials, the response is opposite to the instructed side.\n\n**Why (quoted from CONVERSION_NOTES.md):** \"Correct right trials and incorrect left trials were labeled right. Correct left trials and incorrect right trials were labeled left.\"\n\nThe agent **did not explicitly quote reasoning** for why it chose actual lick direction over instructed side, but this is the standard interpretation of \"lick direction\" -- it refers to what the animal actually did.\n\n---\n\n### 7. behavioral_context Output\n\n**Decision:** WC = 0, DR = 1. Per-trial, constant across time.\n\n**Implementation:** Uses `bp['autowater']` field: autowater=1 is WC (water-cued), autowater=0 is DR (delayed response).\n\n**Why (quoted from WorkingWithDataObjs.m, read by the agent):** \"obj.bp.autowater=1 when water was delivered regardless of animal choice (0 otherwise). this field can be used as a proxy for obtaining water-cued blocks and delayed-response blocks of trials.\"\n\n**Note:** The mapping is **inverted** relative to the task instructions which specify WC=0, DR=1. The agent maps `autowater==1` (WC) to context=0 and `autowater==0` (DR) to context=1. This matches the spec but the code line reads: `context = 0 if bp[\"autowater\"][tr] else 1`.\n\n---\n\n### 8. outcome Output\n\n**Decision:** incorrect = 0, correct = 1. Per-trial, constant across time.\n\n**Implementation:** `outcome = 1 if bp[\"hit\"][tr] else 0`\n\n**Why:** Not explicitly stated beyond matching the decoder task spec. Since only `hit` and `miss` trials are included (ignore trials excluded), this is a clean binary.\n\n---\n\n### 9. tongue_velocity Output\n\n**Variables used:** `top_tongue` and `bottom_tongue` features from the bottom camera (DLC tracking).\n\n**Processing steps:**\n1. Extract x,y positions from DLC output for each trial\n2. Interpolate positions onto the neural time axis using the video-offset-corrected frame times\n3. Compute velocity as `np.gradient()` of each position coordinate\n4. For tongue features, NaN velocities are set to 0 (tongue not visible)\n5. Average the x-velocities and y-velocities of top_tongue and bottom_tongue: `tongue_tip_xvel = 0.5 * (top + bottom)`\n6. Compute speed: `sqrt(xvel^2 + yvel^2)`\n7. Replace remaining NaN with 0\n\n**Thresholding:** Per-session 50th percentile over **positive tongue-speed bins only**.\n\n**Why (quoted):** \"The validator found one real issue: the tongue-velocity target collapsed to a constant class because the reference pipeline sets non-visible tongue velocity to zero, and the session median over all bins becomes zero. I'm patching that target to compute its median over positive tongue-speed bins only, which preserves the reference-aligned velocity trace but avoids a degenerate classifier label.\"\n\n**Why tongue-tip approach (quoted):** \"the main remaining design choice is how to collapse multiple tracked tongue/paw landmarks into single velocity scalars. I'm using the same aligned DLC processing as their MATLAB code, then reducing to tongue-tip speed and mean paw speed before session-wise median binarization, which is the least arbitrary way to satisfy the decoder spec without discarding their tracking geometry.\"\n\n**Alignment:** Same video offset correction as all other behavioral variables: `frameTimes - vidshift - goCue`, where `vidshift = mode(bitcode.bitstart)/fs - mode(bp.ev.bitStart)`.\n\n---\n\n### 10. paw_velocity Output\n\n**Variables used:** `top_paw` and `bottom_paw` features from the bottom camera.\n\n**Processing steps:**\n1. Same position extraction and interpolation as tongue\n2. Compute velocity with `np.gradient()`\n3. For non-tongue features, subtract baseline derivative (`nanmedian` of position diffs) and fill NaN with nearest\n4. Compute speed for each paw landmark: `sqrt(xvel^2 + yvel^2)`\n5. Average: `paw_speed = 0.5 * (top_paw_speed + bottom_paw_speed)`\n6. Replace NaN with 0\n\n**Thresholding:** Per-session 50th percentile over all exported bins (no positive-only filter needed since paw speed is generally non-zero).\n\n**Alignment:** Same video offset correction as above.\n\n**Why:** Same reasoning as tongue -- \"reducing to... mean paw speed before session-wise median binarization.\"\n\n---\n\n### 11. motion_energy Output\n\n**Variables used:** Raw motion energy from `motionEnergy_*.mat` files, plus `moveThresh` stored therein.\n\n**Processing steps:**\n1. Fill edge NaN values with nearest available value (matching `fillmissing(...,'nearest')` in MATLAB)\n\n**Thresholding:** Per-session 50th percentile over all bins of included trials.\n\n**Why (no explicit reasoning quoted):** The agent followed the decoder task specification for 50th percentile discretization. Several approaches: (1) Tongue positions where tongue is not visible: NaN velocities set to 0 (no \"not visible\" class). (2) Non-tongue features: NaN filled with nearest available value (`fill_nearest_1d`), matching MATLAB's `fillmissing(...,'nearest')`. (3) Motion energy NaN at trial edges: filled with nearest value. (4) Trials with completely missing video: skipped in position extraction (positions remain NaN, velocities become 0). (5) If all diffs are NaN, baseline derivative defaults to 0.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef fill_nearest_1d(x):\n    idx = np.flatnonzero(~np.isnan(x))\n    if idx.size == 0:\n        return np.zeros_like(x)\n    out = np.interp(np.arange(x.size), idx.astype(float), x[idx])\n    return out\n```\n\niii.
